191→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"frac_seeded\", [0.0, 0.5])\n   192→@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"n_rep\", [20])\n   193→def test_deterministic_when_seeded(sampler, k: int, vocab_size: int,\n   194→                                   batch_size: int, frac_seeded: float,\n   195→                                   n_rep: int):\n   196→    draft_probs = torch.rand(batch_size, k, vocab_size, distribution,\n   233→    despite sampling from a potentially distinct draft distribution.\n   234→\n   235→    This is done by first creating a random target probability\n   236→    distribution and a random draft probability distribution. We then\n   237→    sample token ids from the rejection sampler using these draft\n   238→    and target distributions. The samples are used to estimate\n   239→    the output probability distribution, which we expect to approximate\n   240→    the target distribution.\n   241→\n   242→    A basic distance metric is used to determine similarity between\n   243→    distributions.\n   244→\n   245→    We expect that as we increase the number of samples,\n   246→    the distance between the observed distribution and the target\n   247→    distribution decreases. To measure this, we compare the distance\n   248→    of the observed distribution against both the target distribution\n   249→    and a uniform random distribution. We expect the distance between\n   250→    the observed distribution and the target distribution to improve\n   251→    much more than the distance improvement between the observed\n   252→    distribution and the random distribution.\n   253→    \"\"\"\n   254→    torch.set_default_device(DEVICE)\n   255→    vocab_size = 10\n   256→    k = 2\n   257→    num_reference_probs = 100\n   258→\n   259→    # Prepare draft, target, and reference probability distributions\n   260→    draft_probs, target_probs = (F.softmax(\n   261→        torch.rand(vocab_size, dtype=torch.float32),\n   262→        dim=-1,\n   263→    ) for _ in range(2))\n   264→    reference_probs = F.softmax(\n   265→        torch.rand(num_reference_probs, vocab_size, dtype=torch.float32),\n   266→        dim=-1,\n   267→    )\n   268→\n   269→    sample_sizes = [10, 100, 1_000, 10_000, 100_000]\n   270→    distance_wrt_reference: list[float] = []\n   271→    distance_wrt_target: list[float] = []\n   272→\n   273→    for num_samples in sample_sizes:\n   274→        # Sample using rejection sampling.\n   275→        rej_sample_probs = estimate_rejection_sampling_pdf(\n   276→            draft_probs, target_probs, k, vocab_size, num_samples)\n   277→        rej_sample_probs = rej_sample_probs.to(DEVICE)\n   278→\n   279→        # Average distance from reference probs.\n   280→        reference_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(\n   281→            reference_probs,\n   282→            rej_sample_probs).item() / reference_probs.shape[0]\n   283→        target_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(target_probs,\n   284→                                              rej_sample_probs).item()\n   285→\n   286→        distance_wrt_reference.append(reference_vs_rejsample_dist)\n   287→        distance_wrt_target.append(target_vs_rejsample_dist)\n   288→\n   289→        relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n   290→            distance_wrt_target)\n   291→        relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n   292→            distance_wrt_reference)\n   293→\n   294→        print(f\"{num_samples=} {target_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f} \"\n   295→              f\"{reference_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f}\")\n   296→        print(f\"{num_samples=} {relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target=:.02f} \"\n   297→              f\"{relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference=:.02f}\")\n   298→\n   299→    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n   300→        distance_wrt_target)\n   301→    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n   302→        distance_wrt_reference)\n   303→\n   304→    expected_improvement_multiplier = 20\n   305→    assert (relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target\n   306→            > relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference *\n   307→            expected_improvement_multiplier)\n   308→\n   309→\n   310→def get_ratio_first_to_last(elements: list[float]) -> float:\n   311→    return elements[0] / elements[-1]\n   312→\n   313→\n   314→def estimate_rejection_sampling_pdf(\n   315→    draft_probs: torch.Tensor,\n   316→    target_probs: torch.Tensor,\n   317→    k: int,\n   318→    vocab_size: int,\n   319→    num_samples: int,\n   320→) -> torch.Tensor:\n   321→    \"\"\"Estimate the probability distribution of the output tokens\n   322→    using rejection sampling.\n   323→\n   324→    Args:\n   325→        draft_probs: Draft probability distribution.\n   326→        target_probs: Target probability distribution.\n   327→        num_samples: Number of samples to draw.\n   328→\n   329→    Returns:\n   330→        Estimated probability distribution of the output tokens.\n   331→    \"\"\"\n   332→    sampler = RejectionSampler()\n   333→    # Repeat draft probs num_samples times.\n   334→    draft_probs = draft_probs.reshape(1, 1,\n   335→                                      vocab_size).repeat(num_samples, k, 1)\n   336→\n   337→    # Repeat target probs num_samples * (k + 1) times.\n   338→    target_probs = target_probs.reshape(1, 1, vocab_size).repeat(\n   339→        num_samples, k + 1, 1).reshape(num_samples * (k + 1), vocab_size)\n   340→\n   341→    # Randomly sample draft token ids from draft probs.\n   342→    draft_token_ids = torch.multinomial(draft_probs[:, 0, :],\n   343→                                        num_samples=k,\n   344→                                        replacement=True).reshape(\n   345→                                            num_samples, k)\n   346→\n   347→    # Bonus tokens not used but required.\n   348→    bonus_token_ids = torch.zeros((num_samples, 1), dtype=torch.int64,\n   349→                                  device=DEVICE)\n   350→\n   351→    sampling_metadata = create_sampling_metadata(all_greedy=False)\n   352→    output_token_ids = sampler(draft_token_ids.tolist(), draft_probs,\n   353→                               bonus_token_ids, target_probs,\n   354→                               sampling_metadata)\n   355→    output_token_ids = output_token_ids[:, :-1].flatten()\n   356→\n   357→    hist = torch.histogram(output_token_ids.to(dtype=torch.float,\n   358→                                               device=\"cpu\"),\n   359→                           bins=vocab_size,\n   360→                           range=(0, vocab_size),\n   361→                           density=True)\n   362→\n   363→    return hist.hist\n   364→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. distribution,\n    despite sampling from a potentially distinct draft distribution.\n\n    This is done by first creating a random target probability\n    distribution and a random draft probability distribution. We then\n    sample token ids from the rejection sampler using these draft\n    and target distributions. The samples are used to estimate\n    the output probability distribution, which we expect to approximate\n    the target distribution.\n\n    A basic distance metric is used to determine similarity between\n    distributions.\n\n    We expect that as we increase the number of samples,\n    the distance between the observed distribution and the target\n    distribution decreases. To measure this, we compare the distance\n    of the observed distribution against both the target distribution\n    and a uniform random distribution. We expect the distance between\n    the observed distribution and the target distribution to improve\n    much more than the distance improvement between the observed\n    distribution and the random distribution.\n    \"\"\"\n    torch.set_default_device(DEVICE)\n    vocab_size = 10\n    k = 2\n    num_reference_probs = 100\n\n    # Prepare draft, target, and reference probability distributions\n    draft_probs, target_probs = (F.softmax(\n        torch.rand(vocab_size, dtype=torch.float32),\n        dim=-1,\n    ) for _ in range(2))\n    reference_probs = F.softmax(\n        torch.rand(num_reference_probs, vocab_size, dtype=torch.float32),\n        dim=-1,\n    )\n\n    sample_sizes = [10, 100, 1_000, 10_000, 100_000]\n    distance_wrt_reference: list[float] = []\n    distance_wrt_target: list[float] = []\n\n    for num_samples in sample_sizes:\n        # Sample using rejection sampling.\n        rej_sample_probs = estimate_rejection_sampling_pdf(\n            draft_probs, target_probs, k, vocab_size, num_samples)\n        rej_sample_probs = rej_sample_probs.to(DEVICE)\n\n        # Average distance from reference probs.\n        reference_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(\n            reference_probs,\n            rej_sample_probs).item() / reference_probs.shape[0]\n        target_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(target_probs,\n                                              rej_sample_probs).item()\n\n        distance_wrt_reference.append(reference_vs_rejsample_dist)\n        distance_wrt_target.append(target_vs_rejsample_dist)\n\n        relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n            distance_wrt_target)\n        relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n            distance_wrt_reference)\n\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} {target_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f} \"\n              f\"{reference_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f}\")\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} {relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target=:.02f} \"\n              f\"{relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference=:.02f}\")\n\n    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n        distance_wrt_target)\n    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n        distance_wrt_reference)\n\n    expected_improvement_multiplier = 20\n    assert (relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target\n            > relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference *\n            expected_improvement_multiplier)\n\n\ndef get_ratio_first_to_last(elements: list[float]) -> float:\n    return elements[0] / elements[-1]\n\n\ndef estimate_rejection_sampling_pdf(\n    draft_probs: torch.Tensor,\n    target_probs: torch.Tensor,\n    k: int,\n    vocab_size: int,\n    num_samples: int,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"Estimate the probability distribution of the output tokens\n    using rejection sampling.\n\n    Args:\n        draft_probs: Draft probability distribution.\n        target_probs: Target probability distribution.\n        num_samples: Number of samples to draw.\n\n    Returns:\n        Estimated probability distribution of the output tokens.\n    \"\"\"\n    sampler = RejectionSampler()\n    # Repeat draft probs num_samples times.\n    draft_probs = draft_probs.reshape(1, 1,\n                                      vocab_size).repeat(num_samples, k, 1)\n\n    # Repeat target probs num_samples * (k + 1) times.\n    target_probs = target_probs.reshape(1, 1, vocab_size).repeat(\n        num_samples, k + 1, 1).reshape(num_samples * (k + 1), vocab_size)\n\n    # Randomly sample draft token ids from draft probs.\n    draft_token_ids = torch.multinomial(draft_probs[:, 0, :],\n                                        num_samples=k,\n                                        replacement=True).reshape(\n                                            num_samples, k)\n\n    # Bonus tokens not used but required.\n    bonus_token_ids = torch.zeros((num_samples, 1), dtype=torch.int64,\n                                  device=DEVICE)\n\n    sampling_metadata = create_sampling_metadata(all_greedy=False)\n    output_token_ids = sampler(draft_token_ids.tolist(), draft_probs,\n                               bonus_token_ids, target_probs,\n                               sampling_metadata)\n    output_token_ids = output_token_ids[:, :-1].flatten()\n\n    hist = torch.histogram(output_token_ids.to(dtype=torch.float,\n                                               device=\"cpu\"),\n                           bins=vocab_size,\n                           range=(0, vocab_size),\n                           density=True)\n\n    return For example, we can use top_p, top_k sampling for\n    32→        bonus tokens, while spec decode does not support these sampling\n    33→        strategies.\n    34→    output tokens: \n    35→        Tokens are finally generated with the rejection sampler. \n    36→        output tokens = accepted tokens + recovered tokens + bonus tokens\n    37→    \"\"\"\n    38→\n    39→    def __init__(self):\n    40→        super().__init__()\n    41→\n    42→    def forward(\n    43→        self,\n    44→        draft_token_ids: list[list[int]],\n    45→        draft_probs: Optional[torch.Tensor],\n    46→        bonus_token_ids_tensor: torch.Tensor,  # [batch_size, 1]\n    47→        target_probs: torch.Tensor,  # [num_total_tokens, vocab_size]\n    48→        sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n    49→    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n    50→        '''\n    51→        Args:\n    52→            draft_token_ids (List[List[int]]):\n    53→                A 2D list of token IDs for each request in the batch. \n    54→                Each request might have different number of draft tokens. \n    55→                It may also contain empty lists for requests that have \n    56→                no draft tokens.\n    57→            draft_probs (Optional[torch.Tensor]):\n    58→                Probability distribution for the draft tokens. [batch_size, max_spec_len + 1, vocab_size]\n   120→        target_probs: torch.Tensor,\n   121→        sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n   122→    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n   123→        # Add 1 to include the 'bonus' token.\n   124→        if sampling_metadata.all_greedy:\n   125→            # Produce a mask that remains 1 (True) until the first\n   126→            # mismatch (cumprod turns 0 after a mismatch).\n   127→            target_token_ids_tensor = target_probs.argmax(dim=-1)\n   128→            accept_mask = (target_token_ids_tensor[:, :-1] ==\n   129→                           draft_token_ids_tensor).cumprod(dim=1)\n   130→\n   131→            # Identify valid positions (non-padding).\n   132→            valid_mask = target_token_ids_tensor != INVALID_TOKEN_ID\n   133→            # Generate mask with bonus token.\n   134→            generate_mask = torch.cat([\n   135→                accept_mask,\n   136→                torch.zeros(accept_mask.size(0), 1, device=accept_mask.device)\n   137→            ],\n   138→                                      dim=1).to(torch.bool) & valid_mask\n   139→            zeros_mask = (generate_mask == 0)\n   140→            first_zero_idx = zeros_mask.float().argmax(dim=1)\n   141→            # Figure out which rows actually contain at least one zero.\n   142→            rows_with_zero = zeros_mask.any(dim=1)\n   143→            # Use indexing to set the first zero in each of those rows to 1.\n   144→            generate_mask[rows_with_zero, first_zero_idx[rows_with_zero]] = 1\n   145→\n   146→            output_token_ids = target_token_ids_tensor\n   147→            output_token_ids[~generate_mask] = INVALID_TOKEN_ID\n   148→        else:\n   149→            # Reference: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.17192\n   150→            # 1. Extract the probabilities of the draft tokens.\n   151→            # [batch_size, max_spec_len]\n   152→            batch_size = draft_token_ids_tensor.size(0)\n   153→            max_spec_len = draft_token_ids_tensor.size(1)\n   154→            invalid_idx = draft_token_ids_tensor == INVALID_TOKEN_ID\n   155→            draft_token_ids_tensor[invalid_idx] = 0\n   156→            assert draft_probs is not None\n   157→            draft_token_probs = draft_probs.gather(\n   158→                dim=-1, index=draft_token_ids_tensor.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n   159→            target_token_probs = target_probs.gather(\n   160→                dim=-1, index=draft_token_ids_tensor.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n   161→            # Force the probabilities of invalid tokens to inf\n   162→            # so that they are not accepted.\n   163→            draft_token_probs[invalid_idx] = float('inf')\n   164→\n   165→            # 2. Sample recovered token ids.\n   194→            recovered_token_ids = random_sample(\n   195→                normalized_bonus_prob,\n   196→                sampling_metadata.generators).reshape(batch_size, max_spec_len)\n   197→\n   198→            # 6. Get the final output token ids.\n   199→            # output_token_ids = accepted_token_ids +\n   200→            #                    recovered_token_ids +\n   201→            #                    bonus_token_id\n   202→            recovered_bonus_token_ids = torch.cat(\n   203→                [recovered_token_ids, bonus_token_ids_tensor], dim=1)\n   204→            # Generate mask with bonus tokens.\n   205→            generate_mask = torch.cat([\n   206→                accept_mask,\n   207→                torch.zeros(batch_size, 1, device=accept_mask.device)\n   208→            ],\n   209→                                      dim=1).to(torch.bool)\n   210→            zeros_mask = (generate_mask == 0)\n   211→            first_zero_idx = zeros_mask.float().argmax(dim=1)\n   212→            output_token_ids = torch.cat([\n   213→                accepted_token_ids,\n   214→                torch.full((batch_size, 1),\n   215→                           fill_value=INVALID_TOKEN_ID,\n   216→                           device=accept_mask.device)\n   217→            ],\n   218→                                         dim=1)\n   219→            output_token_ids[torch.arange(batch_size),\n   220→                             first_zero_idx] = recovered_bonus_token_ids[\n   221→                                 torch.arange(batch_size), first_zero_idx]\n   222→\n   223→        return output_token_ids\n   224→\n   225→    def compute_probs(self, logits: torch.Tensor,\n   226→                      sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n   227→                      sample_lens: list[int]) -> torch.Tensor:\n   228→        \"\"\"\n   229→        Compute probability distribution from logits based on sampling metadata.\n   230→    \n   231→        This function applies temperature scaling to the logits and converts \n   232→        them to probabilities using softmax. Note that division by \n   233→        temperature is not performed inplace to preserve the original logits \n   234→        tensor, which will be used by the original sampler to get bonus tokens.\n   235→        \n   236→        Args:\n   237→            logits: Input logits tensor to be converted to probabilities\n   238→            sampling_metadata: Metadata containing sampling parameters such \n   239→                    as temperature and whether greedy sampling is used\n   240→            sample_lens: List of sample lengths used for repeating \n   241→                    temperature values\n   242→            \n   243→        Returns:\n   244→            torch.Tensor: Probability distribution (softmax of scaled logits) \n   245→                    if non-greedy sampling is used, otherwise returns the \n   246→                    original logits\n   247→        \"\"\"\n   248→        if sampling_metadata.all_greedy:\n   249→            return logits\n   250→        assert sampling_metadata.temperature is not None\n   251→        temps = sampling_metadata.temperature\n   252→        # Move temps to logits device if needed\n   253→        if temps.device != logits.device:\n   254→            temps = temps.to(logits.device)\n   255→        counts = torch.as_tensor(sample_lens, device=temps.device)\n   256→        temperature = torch.repeat_interleave(temps, counts).unsqueeze(1)\n   257→        logits = logits / temperature\n   258→        return logits.softmax(dim=-1, dtype=torch.float32)\n   259→\n   260→\n   261→def _create_greedy_token_probs(\n   262→    token_ids: torch.Tensor,\n   263→    vocab_size: int,\n   264→    out_device: torch.device,\n   265→) -> torch.Tensor:\n   266→    batch_size, num_tokens = token_ids.shape\n   267→\n   268→    token_probs = torch.zeros(batch_size,\n   269→                              num_tokens,\n   270→                              vocab_size,\n   271→                              dtype=torch.float,\n   272→                              device=out_device)\n   273→\n   274→    # [num_total_tokens, vocab_size]\n   288→        sample_lens: list[int]) -> torch.Tensor:\n   289→    \"\"\"\n   290→        Converts a 2D tensor of probabilities to a 3D tensor with padding.\n   291→        [num_total_tokens, vocab_size] -> \n   292→            [batch_size, max_spec_len + 1, vocab_size]\n   293→    \"\"\"\n   294→    cumulative_lens = torch.as_tensor(sample_lens, device=probs.device).cumsum(0)\n   295→    split_indices = cumulative_lens[:-1].tolist()  # Exclude last index\n   296→\n   297→    # Split into chunks without loops\n   298→    chunks = torch.tensor_split(probs, split_indices, dim=0)\n   299→\n   300→    # Pad all sequences to maximum length\n   301→    padded_probs = pad_sequence(chunks, batch_first=True, padding_value=0.0)\n   302→    return padded_probs\n   303→\n   304→\n   305→def _create_uniform_samples(seeded_seqs: dict[int, torch.Generator],\n   306→                            batch_size: int, k: int,\n   307→                            device: torch.device) -> torch.Tensor:\n   308→    \"\"\"\n   309→        Generates a batch of uniform random samples, with optional seeding \n   310→        for specific sequences.\n   311→\n   312→        This method creates a tensor of shape `(batch_size, k)` filled \n   313→        with uniform random values in the range [0, 1). If `seeded_seqs` \n   314→        is provided, the sequences corresponding to specific indices \n   315→        will be generated using the provided `torch.Generator` for \n   316→        reproducibility. The other sequences will be generated without \n   317→        a seed.\n   318→\n   319→        Args:\n   320→            seeded_seqs : Optional[Dict[int, torch.Generator]]\n   321→                A dictionary mapping indices in the batch to \n   322→                `torch.Generator` objects.\n   323→            batch_size : int\n   324→                The number of sequences to generate.\n   325→            k : int\n   326→                The number of random samples per sequence.\n   327→            device : torch.device\n   328→                The device on which to allocate the tensor.\n   329→\n   330→        Returns:\n   331→            uniform_rand : torch.Tensor\n   332→                A tensor of shape `(batch_size, k)` containing uniform \n   333→                random values in the range [0, 1).\n   334→        \"\"\"\n   335→\n   336→    uniform_rand = torch.rand(batch_size,\n   337→                              k,\n   338→                              dtype=torch.float32,\n   339→                              device=device)\n   340→    # Apply seeded generators only where needed\n   341→    if seeded_seqs:\n   342→        for idx, generator in seeded_seqs.items():\n   343→            uniform_rand[idx].uniform_(0, 1, generator=generator)\n   344→    return uniform_rand\n   345→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. The bonus token is only sampled from the target\n        probabilities. We pass in the bonus tokens instead of sampling them\n        in the rejection sampler to allow for more flexibility in the\n        sampling process. For example, we can use top_p, top_k sampling for\n        bonus tokens, while spec decode does not support these sampling\n        strategies.\n    output tokens: \n        Tokens are finally generated with the rejection sampler. \n        output tokens = accepted tokens + recovered tokens + bonus tokens\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        super().__init__()\n\n    def forward(\n        self,\n        draft_token_ids: list[list[int]],\n        draft_probs: Optional[torch.Tensor],\n        bonus_token_ids_tensor: torch.Tensor,  # [batch_size, 1]\n        target_probs: torch.Tensor,  # [num_total_tokens, vocab_size]\n        sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        '''\n        Args:\n            draft_token_ids (List[List[int]]):\n                A 2D list of token IDs for each request in the batch. \n                Each request might have different number of draft tokens. \n                It may also contain empty lists for requests that have \n                no draft tokens.\n            draft_probs (Optional[torch.Tensor]):\n                Probability distribution for the draft tokens. Shape is\n                [batch_size, max_spec_len, vocab_size]. Can be None if \n                probabilities are not provided, which is the case for\n                ngram spec decode.\n            bonus_token_ids_tensor (torch.Tensor):\n                A tensor containing bonus tokens. Shape is [batch_size, 1]. \n                Bonus tokens are added to the end of the sequence if all \n                proposed tokens are accepted. We generate the bonus tokens \n                outside of the rejection sampler with the default sampling \n                strategy. It allows for more flexibility in the sampling \n                process such as top_p, top_k sampling.\n            target_probs (torch.Tensor):\n                Target model probability distribution.\n                Shape is [num_total_tokens, vocab_size]. num_total_tokens \n                is the total number of tokens from all requests. Here, \n                probabilities from different requests are flattened into\n                a single tensor because this is the shape of the output \n                logits.\n            sampling_metadata (SamplingMetadata):\n                Additional metadata needed for sampling, such as temperature,\n                top-k/top-p parameters, or other relevant information.\n        Returns:\n            output_token_ids (torch.Tensor):\n                A tensor containing the final output token IDs.\n        '''\n\n        # NOTE: The following input preparationg can be moved\n        # to the model runner with a persistent manner for better\n        # performance.\n        # Convert draft token IDs to a tensor, split by sample_lens, then pad.\n        draft_token_ids = [\n            torch.tensor(x, dtype=int, device='cpu') for x in draft_token_ids\n        ]\n        draft_token_ids_tensor = pad_sequence(draft_token_ids,\n                                              batch_first=True,\n                                              padding_value=INVALID_TOKEN_ID)\n\n        # NOTE: CPU <-> GPU synchronization happens here.\n        draft_token_ids_tensor = draft_token_ids_tensor.to(target_probs.device)\n\n        # Create one-hot tensor for draft token ids.\n        # This is used for ngram where we don't have draft_probs.\n        if draft_probs is None and not sampling_metadata.all_greedy:\n            vocab_size = target_probs.size(-1)\n            draft_probs = _create_greedy_token_probs(draft_token_ids_tensor,\n                                                     vocab_size,\n                                                     target_probs.device)\n        sample_lens = [len(x) + 1 for x in draft_token_ids]\n        target_probs = _convert_2d_probs(target_probs, sample_lens)\n\n        return self.forward_native(draft_token_ids_tensor, draft_probs,\n                                   bonus_token_ids_tensor, target_probs,\n                                   sampling_metadata)\n\n    # TODO: The following method can be optimized for better performance.\n    def forward_native(\n        self,\n        draft_token_ids_tensor: torch.Tensor,\n        # [batch_size, max_spec_len, vocab_size]\n        draft_probs: Optional[torch.Tensor],\n        bonus_token_ids_tensor: torch.Tensor,\n        # [batch_size, max_spec_len + 1, vocab_size]\n        target_probs: torch.Tensor,\n        sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        # Add 1 to include the 'bonus' token.\n        if sampling_metadata.all_greedy:\n            # Produce a mask that remains 1 (True) until the first\n            # mismatch (cumprod turns 0 after a mismatch).\n            target_token_ids_tensor = target_probs.argmax(dim=-1)\n            accept_mask = (target_token_ids_tensor[:, :-1] ==\n                           draft_token_ids_tensor).cumprod(dim=1)\n\n            # Identify valid positions (non-padding).\n            valid_mask = target_token_ids_tensor != INVALID_TOKEN_ID\n            # Generate mask with bonus token.\n            generate_mask = torch.cat([\n                accept_mask,\n                torch.zeros(accept_mask.size(0), 1, device=accept_mask.device)\n            ],\n                                      dim=1).to(torch.bool) & valid_mask\n            zeros_mask = (generate_mask == 0)\n            first_zero_idx = zeros_mask.float().argmax(dim=1)\n            # Figure out which rows actually contain at least one zero.\n            rows_with_zero = zeros_mask.any(dim=1)\n            # Use indexing to set the first zero in each of those rows to 1.\n            generate_mask[rows_with_zero, first_zero_idx[rows_with_zero]] = 1\n\n            output_token_ids = target_token_ids_tensor\n            output_token_ids[~generate_mask] = INVALID_TOKEN_ID\n        else:\n            # Reference: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.17192\n            # 1. Extract the probabilities of the draft tokens.\n            # [batch_size, max_spec_len]\n            batch_size = draft_token_ids_tensor.size(0)\n            max_spec_len = draft_token_ids_tensor.size(1)\n            invalid_idx = draft_token_ids_tensor == INVALID_TOKEN_ID\n            draft_token_ids_tensor[invalid_idx] = 0\n            assert draft_probs is not None\n            draft_token_probs = draft_probs.gather(\n                dim=-1, index=draft_token_ids_tensor.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n            target_token_probs = target_probs.gather(\n                dim=-1, index=draft_token_ids_tensor.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n            # Force the probabilities of invalid tokens to inf\n            # so that they are not accepted.\n            draft_token_probs[invalid_idx] = float('inf')\n\n            # 2. Generate uniform samples.\n            # [batch_size, max_spec_len + 1]\n            uniform_samples = _create_uniform_samples(\n                sampling_metadata.generators, batch_size, max_spec_len,\n                target_probs.device)\n\n            # 3. Accept or reject the samples.\n            # [batch_size, max_spec_len]\n            # If the draft token probabilities are 0, set them to the smallest\n            # positive normal value representable by float32.\n            safe_draft_probs = torch.where(draft_token_probs > 0,\n                                           draft_token_probs,\n                                           torch.finfo(torch.float32).tiny)\n            accepted = uniform_samples <= target_token_probs / safe_draft_probs\n            accept_mask = accepted.cumprod(dim=1)\n            # Set the token ids to the draft token ids if accepted, otherwise\n            # set them to INVALID_TOKEN_ID.\n            accepted_token_ids = (draft_token_ids_tensor * accept_mask +\n                                  INVALID_TOKEN_ID * (1 - accept_mask))\n\n            # 4. Adjust the distribution for the recovered tokens.\n            # Clamp the bonus probabilities to the smallest positive normal\n            # value representable by float32.\n            bonus_prob = torch.clamp(target_probs[:, :-1, :] - draft_probs,\n                                     min=torch.finfo(torch.float32).tiny)\n            normalized_bonus_prob = bonus_prob / bonus_prob.sum(dim=-1,\n                                                                keepdim=True)\n\n            # 5. Sample recovered token ids.\n            recovered_token_ids = random_sample(\n                normalized_bonus_prob,\n                sampling_metadata.generators).reshape(batch_size, max_spec_len)\n\n            # 6. Get the final output token ids.\n            # output_token_ids = accepted_token_ids +\n            #                    recovered_token_ids +\n            #                    bonus_token_id\n            recovered_bonus_token_ids = torch.cat(\n                [recovered_token_ids, bonus_token_ids_tensor], dim=1)\n            # Generate mask with bonus tokens.\n            generate_mask = torch.cat([\n                accept_mask,\n                torch.zeros(batch_size, 1, device=accept_mask.device)\n            ],\n                                      dim=1).to(torch.bool)\n            zeros_mask = (generate_mask == 0)\n            first_zero_idx = zeros_mask.float().argmax(dim=1)\n            output_token_ids = torch.cat([\n                accepted_token_ids,\n                torch.full((batch_size, 1),\n                           fill_value=INVALID_TOKEN_ID,\n                           device=accept_mask.device)\n            ],\n                                         dim=1)\n            output_token_ids[torch.arange(batch_size),\n                             first_zero_idx] = recovered_bonus_token_ids[\n                                 torch.arange(batch_size), first_zero_idx]\n\n        return output_token_ids\n\n    def compute_probs(self, logits: torch.Tensor,\n                      sampling_metadata: SamplingMetadata,\n                      sample_lens: list[int]) -> torch.Tensor:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute probability distribution from logits based on sampling metadata.\n    \n        This function applies temperature scaling to the logits and converts \n        them to probabilities using softmax. Note that division by \n        temperature is not performed inplace to preserve the original logits \n        tensor, which will be used by the original sampler to get bonus tokens.\n        \n        Args:\n            logits: Input logits tensor to be converted to probabilities\n            sampling_metadata: Metadata containing sampling parameters such \n                    as temperature and whether greedy sampling is used\n            sample_lens: List of sample lengths used for repeating \n                    temperature values\n            \n        Returns:\n            torch.Tensor: Probability distribution (softmax of scaled logits) \n                    if non-greedy sampling is used, otherwise returns the \n                    original logits\n        \"\"\"\n        if sampling_metadata.all_greedy:\n            return logits\n        assert sampling_metadata.temperature is not None\n        temps = sampling_metadata.temperature\n        # Move temps to logits device if needed\n        if temps.device != logits.device:\n            temps = temps.to(logits.device)\n        counts = torch.as_tensor(sample_lens, device=temps.device)\n        temperature = torch.repeat_interleave(temps, counts).unsqueeze(1)\n        logits = logits / temperature\n        return logits.softmax(dim=-1, dtype=torch.float32)\n\n\ndef _create_greedy_token_probs(\n    token_ids: torch.Tensor,\n    vocab_size: int,\n    out_device: torch.device,\n) -> torch.Tensor:\n    batch_size, num_tokens = token_ids.shape\n\n    token_probs = torch.zeros(batch_size,\n                              num_tokens,\n                              vocab_size,\n                              dtype=torch.float,\n                              device=out_device)\n\n    # Ignore INVALID_TOKEN_ID.\n    valid_mask = (token_ids != INVALID_TOKEN_ID)\n    valid_indices = token_ids.clone()\n    valid_indices[~valid_mask] = 0\n\n    token_probs.scatter_(dim=2,\n                         index=valid_indices.unsqueeze(-1),\n                         src=valid_mask.unsqueeze(-1).float())\n\n    return token_probs\n\n\ndef _convert_2d_probs(\n        probs: torch.Tensor,  # [num_total_tokens, vocab_size]\n        sample_lens: list[int]) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"\n        Converts a 2D tensor of probabilities to a 3D tensor with padding.\n        [num_total_tokens, vocab_size] -> \n            [batch_size, max_spec_len + 1, vocab_size]\n    \"\"\"\n    cumulative_lens = torch.as_tensor(sample_lens, device=probs.device).cumsum(0)\n    split_indices = cumulative_lens[:-1].tolist()  # Exclude last index\n\n    # Split into chunks without loops\n    chunks = torch.tensor_split(probs, split_indices, dim=0)\n\n    # Pad all sequences to maximum length\n    padded_probs = pad_sequence(chunks, batch_first=True, padding_value=0.0)\n    return padded_probs\n\n\ndef _create_uniform_samples(seeded_seqs: dict[int, torch.Generator],\n                            batch_size: int, k: int,\n                            device: torch.device) -> torch.Tensor:\n    \"\"\"\n        Generates a batch of uniform random samples, with optional seeding \n        for specific sequences.\n\n        This method creates a tensor of shape `(batch_size, k)` filled \n        with uniform random values in the range [0, 1). If `seeded_seqs` \n        is provided, the sequences corresponding to specific indices \n        will be generated using the provided `torch.Generator` for \n        reproducibility. The other sequences will be generated without \n        a seed.\n\n        Args:\n            seeded_seqs : Optional[Dict[int, torch.Generator]]\n                A dictionary mapping indices in the batch to \n                `torch.Generator` objects.\n            batch_size : int\n                The number of sequences to generate.\n            k : int\n                The number of random samples per sequence.\n            device : torch.device\n                The device on which to allocate the tensor.\n\n        Returns:\n            uniform_rand : torch.Tensor\n                A tensor of shape `(batch_size, k)` containing uniform \n                random values in the range [0, 1).\n        \"\"\"\n\n    uniform_rand = torch.rand(batch_size,\n                              k,\n                              dtype=torch.float32,\n                              device=device)\n    # Apply seeded generators only where needed\n    if seeded_seqs:\n        for idx, generator in seeded_seqs.items():\n            uniform_rand[idx].uniform_(0, 1, generator=generator)\n    return uniform_rand\n","numLines":345,"startLine":1,"totalLines":345}}}

distribution. We then\n    sample token ids from the rejection sampler using these draft\n    and target distributions. The samples are used to estimate\n    the output probability distribution, which we expect to approximate\n    the target distribution.\n\n    A basic distance metric is used to determine similarity between\n    distributions.\n\n    We expect that as we increase the number of samples,\n    the distance between the observed distribution and the target\n    distribution decreases. To measure this, we compare the distance\n    of the observed distribution against both the target distribution\n    and a uniform random distribution. We expect the distance between\n    the observed distribution and the target distribution to improve\n    much more than the distance improvement between the observed\n    distribution and the random distribution.\n    \"\"\"\n    torch.set_default_device(DEVICE)\n    vocab_size = 10\n    k = 2\n    num_reference_probs = 100\n\n    # Prepare draft, target, and reference probability distributions\n draft_probs, target_probs, k, vocab_size, num_samples)\n        rej_sample_probs = rej_sample_probs.to(DEVICE)\n\n        # Average distance from reference probs.\n        reference_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(\n            reference_probs,\n            rej_sample_probs).item() / reference_probs.shape[0]\n        target_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(target_probs,\n                                              rej_sample_probs).item()\n\n        distance_wrt_reference.append(reference_vs_rejsample_dist)\n        distance_wrt_target.append(target_vs_rejsample_dist)\n\n distance_wrt_reference)\n\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} {target_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f} \"\n              f\"{reference_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f}\")\n        print(f\"{num_samples=}   distance_wrt_target)\n    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n        distance_wrt_reference)\n\n    expected_improvement_multiplier = 20\n    assert distribution. We then\n    sample token ids from the rejection sampler using these draft\n    and target distributions. The samples are used to estimate\n    the output probability distribution, which we expect to approximate\n    the target distribution.\n\n    A basic distance metric is used to determine similarity between\n    distributions.\n\n    We expect that as we increase the number of samples,\n    the distance between the observed distribution and the target\n    distribution decreases. To measure this, we compare the distance\n    of the observed distribution against both the target distribution\n    and a uniform random distribution. We expect the distance between\n    the observed distribution and the target distribution to improve\n    much more than the distance improvement between the observed\n    distribution and the random draft_probs, target_probs, k, vocab_size, num_samples)\n        rej_sample_probs = rej_sample_probs.to(DEVICE)\n\n        # Average distance from reference probs.\n        reference_vs_rejsample_dist distance_wrt_reference)\n\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} {target_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f} \"\n              f\"{reference_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f}\")\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} distribution,\n    despite sampling from a potentially distinct draft distribution.\n\n    This is done by first creating a random target probability\n    distribution and a random draft probability distribution. We then\n    sample token ids from the rejection sampler using these draft\n    and target distributions. The samples are used to estimate\n    the output probability distribution, which we expect to approximate\n    the target distribution.\n\n    A basic distance metric is used to determine similarity between\n    distributions.\n\n    We expect that as we increase the number of samples,\n    the distance between the observed distribution and the target\n    distribution decreases. To measure this, we compare the distance\n    of the observed distribution against both the target distribution\n    and a uniform random distribution. We expect the distance between\n    the observed distribution and the target distribution to improve\n    much more than the distance improvement between the observed\n    distribution and the random distribution.\n    \"\"\"\n    torch.set_default_device(DEVICE)\n    vocab_size = 10\n    k = 2\n    num_reference_probs = 100\n\n    # Prepare draft, target, and reference probability distributions\n torch.rand(num_reference_probs, vocab_size, dtype=torch.float32),\n        dim=-1,\n    )\n\n    sample_sizes = [10, 100, 1_000, 10_000, 100_000]\n    distance_wrt_reference: list[float] = []\n    distance_wrt_target: list[float] = []\n\n    for num_samples in sample_sizes:\n        # Sample using rejection sampling.\n        rej_sample_probs = estimate_rejection_sampling_pdf(\n            draft_probs, target_probs, k, vocab_size, num_samples)\n        rej_sample_probs = rej_sample_probs.to(DEVICE)\n\n        # Average distance from reference probs.\n        reference_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(\n            reference_probs,\n            rej_sample_probs).item() / reference_probs.shape[0]\n        target_vs_rejsample_dist = torch.dist(target_probs,\n                                              rej_sample_probs).item()\n\n        distance_wrt_reference.append(reference_vs_rejsample_dist)\n        distance_wrt_target.append(target_vs_rejsample_dist)\n\n        relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n            distance_wrt_target)\n        relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n            distance_wrt_reference)\n\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} {target_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f} \"\n              f\"{reference_vs_rejsample_dist=:.05f}\")\n        print(f\"{num_samples=} {relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target=:.02f} \"\n              f\"{relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference=:.02f}\")\n\n    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n        distance_wrt_target)\n    relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference = get_ratio_first_to_last(\n        distance_wrt_reference)\n\n    expected_improvement_multiplier = 20\n    assert (relative_change_in_distance_wrt_target\n            > relative_change_in_distance_wrt_reference *\n            expected_improvement_multiplier)\n\n\ndef get_ratio_first_to_last(elements: list[float]) -> float:\n    return elements[0] / elements[-1]\n\n\ndef estimate_rejection_sampling_pdf(\n    draft_probs: torch.Tensor,\n    target_probs: torch.Tensor,\n    k: int,\n    vocab_size: int,\n vocab_size).repeat(num_samples, k, 1)\n\n    # Repeat target probs num_samples * (k + 1) times.\n    target_probs = target_probs.reshape(1, 1, vocab_size).repeat(\n        num_samples, k + 1, index 000000000..b24388500
--- /dev/null
+++ b/vllm/v1/sample/ops/utils.py
@@ -0,0 +1,35 @@
+# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
+import torch
+
+
+def compute_accept_mask(
+    target_token_ids: torch.Tensor,
+    draft_token_ids: torch.Tensor,
+) -> torch.Tensor:
+    """
+    Compute the accept mask for rejection sampling.
+    
+    Args:
+        target_token_ids: Target token IDs tensor
+        draft_token_ids: Draft token IDs tensor
+        
+    Returns:
+        Accept mask tensor
+    """
+    accept_mask = (target_token_ids == draft_token_ids).cumprod(dim=1)
+    return accept_mask
+
+
+def create_zeros_mask(size: tuple, device: torch.device, dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bool) -> torch.Tensor:
+    """
+    Create a zeros mask efficiently.
+    
+    Args:
+        size: Shape of the tensor
+        device: Device to create tensor on
+        dtype: Data type of the tensor
+        
+    Returns:
+        Zero-filled tensor
+    """
+    return torch.zeros(size, device=device, dtype=dtype)

## Analysis Instructions

Please provide a comprehensive analysis of this agent run. Your analysis should be rigorous and suitable for inclusion in an academic paper.

### 1. Quantitative Assessment

Count and categorize the following from the agent output:

- **Tool Calls**: Count each type of tool/action used (bash commands, file edits, file reads, etc.)
- **Errors**: Count errors by category (syntax, runtime, API, timeout)
- **Iterations**: Count edit-test cycles (how many times the agent modified code and tested)
- **Exploration Steps**: Count how many steps were spent exploring vs. executing

### 2. Tool Usage Analysis

Analyze the logs to provide exact counts of tool usage. Group tools as follows:
- **bash**: Shell commands (ls, cd, python, etc.)
- **read**: File reading operations (Read, etc.)
- **editor**: File modification operations (Write, Edit, NotebookEdit, TodoWrite, etc.)
- **web_search**: Web search operations (WebSearch, BraveSearch, etc.)
- **search**: Codebase search operations (Grep, Glob, Find, etc.)
- **other**: Any other tools not matching above
- **other_details**: A dictionary mapping specific "other" tool names to their counts.
- **discussion**: Briefly explain if the tool usage seems matched to the counts, especially for "other".

### 3. Quality Scores (0-10 scale with detailed justification)

**IMPORTANT CALIBRATION GUIDELINES:**
- **0-2**: Complete failure - no meaningful attempt or catastrophic errors
- **3-4**: Poor - significant gaps, major mistakes, largely ineffective
- **5-6**: Average - partial success, some correct decisions but notable weaknesses
- **7-8**: Good - mostly correct approach with minor issues, solid execution
- **9**: Excellent - nearly optimal, only minor imperfections
- **10**: Exceptional - reserve ONLY for truly outstanding performance that exceeds expectations; should be rare (<5% of runs)

**Scoring discipline:**
- A score of 10/10 requires flawless execution AND going beyond requirements
- Most successful runs should score 6-8, not 9-10
- Be critical and specific about deficiencies even for good runs
- Consider what an ideal agent would do, not just whether the task was completed

For each score, provide:
- The numeric score (0-10, calibrated per above guidelines)
- A detailed justification explaining why you gave this score
- Specific evidence from the agent output

#### Code Understanding Score
Sub-components:
- Repository Navigation (0-10): How well did the agent explore and understand the codebase structure?
- Function Identification (0-10): Did the agent correctly identify the relevant functions to modify?
- Dependency Awareness (0-10): Did the agent understand how different parts of the code interact?

#### Task Alignment Score
Sub-components:
- Goal Comprehension (0-10): Did the agent correctly understand what optimization was needed?
- Constraint Adherence (0-10): Did the agent respect all given constraints?
- Output Relevance (0-10): Were the changes relevant to the performance goal?

#### Approach Quality Score
Sub-components:
- Strategy Coherence (0-10): Was there a clear, logical strategy?
- Exploration Efficiency (0-10): Was exploration focused or scattered?
- Decision Quality (0-10): Were key decisions well-reasoned?

#### Execution Quality Score
Sub-components:
- Edit Correctness (0-10): Were edits syntactically and semantically correct?
- Test Verification (0-10): Did the agent verify that changes worked?
- Error Recovery (0-10): How well did the agent handle failures?

### 3. Categorical Classification

Classify the agent's behavior:

#### Approach Category
Choose ONE:
- `systematic`: Methodical, step-by-step approach with clear plan
- `direct_edit`: Quick targeted changes with minimal exploration
- `exploration_heavy`: Extensive codebase exploration before acting
- `trial_error`: Iterative attempts without clear strategy
- `minimal`: Minimal viable changes, possibly incomplete

#### Tool Usage Pattern
Choose ONE:
- `bash_heavy`: Primarily used bash/shell commands
- `editor_focused`: Primarily used file editing tools
- `read_heavy`: Primarily read files without much editing
- `balanced`: Balanced mix of different tools

#### Failure Category (if the run failed)
Choose ONE or `none` if successful:
- `understanding`: Agent misunderstood the task or codebase
- `execution`: Agent understood but failed to execute correctly
- `timeout`: Agent ran out of time or steps
- `api_error`: Technical/API failures interrupted the agent
- `no_patch`: Agent completed but produced no meaningful changes
- `none`: The run was successful

### 4. Detailed Analysis

#### Key Decisions (list 3-5)
Identify the most important decisions the agent made and explain their impact.

#### Optimization Techniques Used
List any performance optimization techniques the agent attempted (e.g., algorithm changes, memory optimization, parallelization).

#### Missed Opportunities
What could the agent have done better? What obvious optimizations did it miss?

#### Error Recovery Strategy
How did the agent respond to errors? Was the recovery effective?

### 5. Recommendations

- What changes to the agent's prompting might improve performance?
- What additional capabilities would help this type of task?
- What patterns should be encouraged or discouraged?
